Infant Bleeding Cases Surge as More Parents Refuse Vitamin K Shots for Newborns

📅 Nov 09, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Follow Healthline on our socials for all things health.

Two large studies published on November 5 link declining newborn vitamin K shot rates to a sharp increase in infant bleeding cases, including potentially fatal brain hemorrhages.

A research letter, published in JAMA Pediatrics, analyzed data on approximately 11 million infants from 2017 to 2025 and found that vitamin K administration declined from 97.7% to 95%, while cases of vitamin K deficiency bleeding (VKDB) nearly quadrupled.

A second study published in JAMA, based on data from 5.2 million U.S. infants, found that refusal rates more than doubled, rising from 3.1% in 2017 to 6.8% in 2025. Infants who did not receive the shot had a 47% higher risk of bleeding and more than twice the risk of intracranial bleeding.

Additionally, new data from Truveta suggest the trend is continuing: refusals of vitamin K shots increased by 57% between January and June 2026, rising from 5.2% to 8.1% of newborns.

Vitamin K is essential for blood clotting, and newborns naturally have very low levels. Without the preventive injection, infants are at risk for VKDB, which can cause sudden bleeding in the brain, gastrointestinal tract, and other organs. Late-onset VKDB can occur up to six months after birth, often without warning signs, and may be fatal.

The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) and American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) continue to recommend an intramuscular vitamin K injection for all newborns, ideally within 6 hours of birth.

The shot is not a vaccine; it is a nutrient injection that helps prevent dangerous bleeding. A single dose reduces the risk of late-onset VKDB by approximately 98%.

Experts not involved in the studies attribute the rise in refusals partly to broader vaccine hesitancy and mixed public health messaging.

“The decision to vaccinate a newborn or allow a vitamin K injection at birth is complex, with multiple factors playing a role in the ultimate decision,” said Graham Tse, MD, a pediatrician and chief medical officer of MemorialCare Miller Children’s & Women’s Hospital in Long Beach, CA.

Some of these factors, Tse said, include conflicting messages from federal health agencies such as the Department of Health and Human Services (HHS) and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), both overseen by HHS Secretary Robert F. Kennedy, Jr., an outspoken critic of the childhood immunization schedule.

The vitamin K shot is not a vaccine, yet RFK Jr. has declined to reassure parents about its safety, despite the CDC’s current recommendation to administer it at birth.

Additionally, growing medical distrust among parents has led to refusal of routine newborn care, such as the vitamin K shot, Reuters reports.

Healthline spoke with Tse to learn more about the risks of refusing the vitamin K shot and what new or expectant parents should know about this routine newborn treatment.

This interview has been lightly edited and condensed for clarity and length.

Tse: The decision to vaccinate a newborn or allow a vitamin K injection at birth is complex, with multiple factors playing a role in the ultimate decision.

A contributing factor to the decline in vitamin K injection at birth, as well as in newborn hepatitis B vaccination, is the conflicting messages from the Department of Health and Human Services (HHS), and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC).

Recommendations from these entities and others are conflicting or do not align with established, research-based, and proven recommendations from organizations such as the American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) and the Infectious Diseases Society of America.

When there are conflicting recommendations, it creates confusion and doubt among parents about what the best or right choice is for their child.

As well, there is so much misinformation on the internet, among influencers, and on social media that it is difficult to know what information is accurate, well-researched, and proven, versus what may be just someone’s opinion or belief.

Some are mistaking the vitamin K injection at birth for a vaccination, which it is not. Ultimately, all these factors are contributing to parents declining or delaying vitamin K injections at birth.

Tse: Declining the newborn vitamin K injection puts the newborn at significant risk of serious and life threatening complications from vitamin K-deficient bleeding (VKDB). This was previously referred to as hemorrhagic disease of the newborn.

VKDB can be as mild as bruising of the skin but can be as serious as bleeding into the brain, and newborns and infants can die from VKDB.

Declining vitamin K injection at birth is significant because of the serious and potentially life threatening consequences of deficiency of Vitamin K and not being able to clot the blood.

The vitamin K injection at birth is highly effective at preventing VKDB.

Tse: If a neonate or infant has vitamin K deficiency, the ability to clot their blood and stop bleeding is highly reduced.

All newborns are at risk of low vitamin K due to low body stores, low vitamin K content of breast milk, reduced ability of the gut to mobilize vitamin K, and poor transfer of vitamin K across the placenta.

When the blood cannot clot, bleeding from minor or minimal trauma can occur, and even bleeding without injury or trauma may occur. This can include bruises, bleeding from the mouth or gastrointestinal tract, bleeding from cuts or circumcision.

Bleeding can occur anywhere in the body, including in the brain, which can be catastrophic.

VKDB can occur from birth and through several months of life. Bleeding in the brain from VKDB, one of the most serious consequences, can occur at any time from birth to up to 6 months or so and can occur unexpectedly and suddenly. Vitamin K given at birth can prevent this risk of bleeding.

Tse: Vitamin K is a necessary vitamin that all humans need. Vitamin K is not a vaccination.

Newborns need vitamin K to prevent serious, significant, and often catastrophic bleeding during their first several months of life.

Vitamin K has been given to newborns in the United States for close to 70 years, is safe, is studied, and is proven to help prevent vitamin K-deficient bleeding.

I urge new parents to allow their newborns to receive the vitamin K injection at birth — it protects them, keeps them healthy, is proven safe, and is recommended.

SQLPad admin and service tokens concentrate database reach

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A SQL console can be more sensitive than the databases it reaches. SQLPad stores connection definitions and lets users run queries, while administrators can manage users and connections [1]. A service token created by an administrator can turn a browser convenience into a durable API credential with broad database reach.

SQLPad supports local authentication with email and password by default. Passwords are stored in its embedded database using bcrypt hashing. The initial administrator is configured through SQLPAD_ADMIN, optionally with SQLPAD_ADMIN_PASSWORD, and additional users are added by an administrator [1].

The application has two roles today, Admin and Editor. An administrator can add or edit database connections and create users. The configuration documentation says service tokens are generated by logging in as an admin and using the Service Tokens page [2]. Those tokens deserve separate review because they may outlive a browser session and are intended for automation.

I ran three count-only probes on 2026-11-09 with sub_type=all, page 1, and page size 1. The application-fingerprint query app="SQLPad" matched 1,067. The title query title="SQLPad" matched 1,022. A body query http.body="SQLPad" matched 1,084.

The three counts are close, which is useful but not proof that every match is a working SQLPad instance. The app fingerprint is the strongest discovery signal in this run. The count still does not show whether authentication is enabled, which databases are connected, or who holds a service token.

Inventory every connection, its database role, network path, and owner. A SQLPad administrator may be able to edit a connection string even if they do not know the database password. The connection can use a privileged database account, so SQLPad's own role check is not the only authorization layer.

Prefer a dedicated read-only database account for analytic work. Use a separate connection for a job that needs writes. Do not let an Editor share a connection whose credentials can modify every tenant. Query-level controls, database roles, and row-level policies should remain in force.

Review the SQLPAD_USERPASS_AUTH_DISABLED and default-role settings. The documentation warns that disabling local user-password authentication can require a default role for users from another identity source [1]. A misconfigured default of Admin turns authentication success into administrative authority.

The API overview documents HTML Basic authentication for users and password access unless local authentication is disabled. Service tokens are a separate mechanism. Keep tokens in a secret manager, scope each one to a dedicated operator, and record the purpose and owner.

Test the API with a token that should be denied. Confirm that the denial is enforced by SQLPad and by the database account, not only by the UI. Review token creation and last-use records. Revoke stale tokens after integration or staff changes.

Restrict the web and API paths to a management network or a reverse proxy with strong authentication. Require TLS. Protect the embedded database because it contains password hashes, connections, and session state. Back it up with encryption and restrict restore access.

Log user, connection, query identifier, row count, and result status without storing the query text or returned data in an exposed log. Alert on queries that read unusually large volumes, connection changes, new administrators, and service-token creation.

If an administrator account is compromised, disable it, revoke its sessions and service tokens, and review connection changes. Determine which database credentials were visible and rotate them. Review the databases' own audit logs because SQLPad may not record every statement or data access.

The external SQLPad count is a discovery metric. It does not say whether a console is internet-facing, which databases are connected, or whether a token has broad authority. Review those facts at the owner, then use the count to organize outreach and follow-up.

SteamVR vient de rendre la VR plus nette. Voici ce que j'y mettrais.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Cette semaine, Valve a publié une mise à jour bêta de SteamVR qui améliore la qualité d'image transmise à tous les casques autonomes compatibles Steam Link : Meta Quest, HTC Vive Focus, PICO. Steam Link, c'est l'application de streaming de SteamVR — celle qui fait tourner votre bibliothèque Steam sans fil sur un casque autonome. La nouveauté porte sur la netteté du flux.

Vous me direz : encore une note de version. Sauf que cette note touche exactement le point qui bloquait un usage de la VR dont on parle peu.

Depuis des années, la VR se vend comme un média de jeu. Pendant ce temps, une petite communauté l'utilise pour regarder des molécules. Et cette communauté butte sur un problème très concret : lire des détails fins dans un casque. Une hélice alpha, un feuillet bêta, une chaîne latérale qui pointe vers un ligand — ce sont des objets de quelques ångströms. Si le rendu n'est pas net, la structure devient une masse colorée.

C'est un problème de pipeline, pas de puissance brute. Un flux Steam Link, c'est une chaîne : rendu côté PC, encodage, transport réseau, décodage dans le casque, reprojection. Chaque maillon ajoute sa perte. La compression vidéo lisse les arêtes fines. Le sous-échantillonnage chromatique bave sur les lignes de 1 à 2 pixels. Et une liaison peptidique, dans un rendu cartoon à distance de lecture, c'est souvent une ligne de 1 à 2 pixels. Ajoutez le mouvement de tête et la reprojection : les atomes se dédoublent dès que vous tournez.

Rien de tout cela n'est propre à la biologie structurale. Mais c'est là que ça fait le plus mal, parce que la donnée elle-même est dense. Un fichier PDB courant, c'est des milliers d'atomes, des milliers de liaisons, des feuillets superposés sur quelques ångströms de profondeur. Sur un écran 2D, on zoome et on tourne avec la souris. Dans un casque, on tourne la tête — et si l'image n'est pas stable, le cerveau décroche. La charge cognitive part dans la reconstruction de l'image au lieu de la compréhension de la structure.

Chaque amélioration de la qualité d'image rend donc une question plus crédible : et si le bon contenu pour un casque n'était pas un jeu ?

NexoPeek est un laboratoire moléculaire VR pour Meta Quest. Trois couches.

Les visites guidées. 12 visites narrées sur 6 piliers : Biotechnologie de base, Fondements de la vie, Voies et interactions, Médicaments en action, Technologies génétiques, Biotech verte. On entre dans l'hémoglobine pour voir l'oxygène se fixer. On suit le glucose qui se décompose dans la glycolyse. On regarde l'aspirine bloquer les enzymes COX. On assiste à CRISPR-Cas9 qui édite l'ADN.

Le lecteur PDB professionnel. Une recherche intelligente qui résout un nom de gène sur RCSB PDB, UniProtKB, AlphaFold DB, SWISS-MODEL et PDBe, et charge la structure à taille humaine. Les presets Mol* se déclenchent en un geste : cartoon, ball-and-stick, spacefill CPK, surface moléculaire, carte de facteur B, tracé en tube. Les fichiers locaux (.pdb, .cif, .mol2) s'ouvrent avec cartes de champ électrostatique et volumes de densité électronique.

Le banc d'analyse. Attraper, mettre à l'échelle, faire tourner la molécule à mains nues ou aux manettes. La prédiction de poches par IA met en évidence un site druggable (PrankWeb / P2Rank, cloud ou moteur hors ligne). Foldseek superpose un homologue. L'inspecteur de ligands trace les liaisons hydrogène et va chercher un ligand 3D dans PubChem. Deux atomes piqués, et la distance en ångströms s'affiche. Les pastilles de résidus touchées dans la séquence projettent un halo 3D sur la structure.

Et le mode Room View fait flotter la molécule dans votre pièce, menu mural toujours opérationnel.

Deux choix valent d'être explicités, parce qu'ils sont discutables.

Le premier : la recherche fédérée. On ne stocke pas de base locale. On interroge RCSB PDB, UniProtKB, AlphaFold DB, SWISS-MODEL et PDBe, puis on résout un nom de gène vers un identifiant de structure. Le compromis est clair — on dépend du réseau et des quotas des API publiques, mais on hérite de bases maintenues par d'autres, et on ne se retrouve jamais avec une copie périmée. Pour un outil éducatif, c'est le bon arbitrage.

Le deuxième : les presets de rendu. Mol* est puissant, mais sa surface d'API est large. On a préféré exposer des presets à un geste — cartoon, ball-and-stick, spacefill CPK, surface, carte de facteur B, tube — plutôt qu'un panneau de réglages complet. C'est une concession : on perd en expressivité ce qu'on gagne en accessibilité. Pour le public visé, ça se défend.

Côté analyse, la prédiction de poches tourne soit dans le cloud, soit sur un moteur hors ligne. Le mode hors ligne existe pour les environnements sans réseau fiable — salles de cours, labos cloisonnés. Là encore, un arbitrage : moins de modèles disponibles, mais aucune dépendance externe.

Un point sur lequel je n'ai pas de chiffres honnêtes à donner : le gain réel de la mise à jour SteamVR sur une session NexoPeek. Je n'ai pas fait de banc d'essai contrôlé, et je ne vais pas inventer un pourcentage. Ce que je peux dire, c'est que la netteté du flux est le facteur limitant côté casque autonome, et que toute amélioration de ce maillon se ressent d'abord sur les contenus denses en détails fins. La biologie structurale en fait partie.

Parce que l'actualité SteamVR n'est pas un argument de vente — c'est un signal. La VR grand public améliore lentement ce qui comptait le plus pour un usage scientifique : la fidélité visuelle. De l'autre côté, la biologie structurale produit des volumes de données que personne ne peut vraiment lire en 2D.

Ces deux courbes se rapprochent. Je pense que ça vaut la peine de le regarder de près.

NexoPeek est disponible sur le store Meta Quest : https://www.meta.com/experiences/nexopeek/1453797551140347/

NexoPeek est un logiciel éducatif et de visualisation pour la recherche. Il ne constitue ni un avis médical, ni un diagnostic, ni un traitement. Les aides analytiques (prédiction de poches, alignement) assistent l'utilisateur et ne remplacent jamais des pipelines scientifiques validés ni le jugement professionnel.

RTC Room Tokens Explained: 6 Identity and Permission Controls for Healthtech

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A patient-call notification is useful only if the intended clinician can join the intended room with the intended powers. Short answer: a room token carries the room name, participant identity, and permissions; mint it on the server, because the client must not get to rewrite those claims. Treat a permission change as a new-token event, not a UI toggle.

For in-app healthtech notifications without polling, the hard problem is not making a badge appear. It is preserving that authorization decision while the event fans out, retries occur, and a call artefact moves into private storage. I use six controls for that boundary. They are deliberately boring. Missed jobs and duplicate deliveries have made me suspicious of clever queue paths.

Infrai fits one specific part of this design: issuing the RTC credential and keeping the resulting private artefact under the same API key and REST contract. Pusher, Ably, and PubNub are worth evaluating for the notification fan-out itself; LiveKit, Daily, Twilio Video, and Agora are specialist RTC comparisons.

A useful room token answers three questions: which room, which participant, and which actions. The room binds the credential to one call. Identity gives moderation and audit records a stable subject. Permissions decide what that subject may do.

Suppose a care coordinator receives an event for room consult-4821. The browser may display a Join button, but that button is not authority. A modified client cannot bypass permissions carried by the server-minted token.

Never mint this credential in the browser or mobile app. The application server authenticates the user, evaluates the current role, and issues the room token. Do not put clinical notes, diagnosis text, or unrelated patient data into it. None is needed to answer the three authorization questions.

Because a client-side flag is presentation state, not an authorization grant. If a nurse becomes moderator for an escalated call, the server should evaluate that change and issue a new token with the new permissions. The old token does not become stronger because the UI changed.

Identity is also the join point for operations. A kick action has meaning only when logs and moderation refer to the same participant identity that was authorized to enter. Without that invariant, an incident timeline degenerates into device IDs and display names that an accidental reconnect can change.

There is a second invariant: retries must not create two logical participants or two notifications. The fan-out transport may redeliver. Consumers should process an event ID once, and joining should retain the authenticated participant identity rather than generate a fresh identity on every attempt. Assume at-least-once delivery at the application edge. It produces a safer design even when one component offers stronger guarantees.

Push the in-app notification after the server has made the access decision, and include an opaque event ID plus the room reference the recipient needs. Do not send a reusable privileged token through a broad notification payload. Fetch or issue the room token at the authenticated join boundary.

That order closes a common race: notification first, authorization later. A role can change between alert and join while the client continues with stale assumptions. Server issuance turns the latest decision into the credential. If permissions change again, issue another token.

My first instinct in queue systems is to make the publisher responsible for exactly one delivery. That is the wrong place to spend confidence. Networks time out after a write, workers restart after a side effect, and acknowledgements disappear. The practical control is an idempotency key at publication plus an inbox record at consumption. Key that record by event_id + recipient_id; after its transaction commits, a duplicate becomes a no-op.

Consider the ugly sequence rather than the happy path. The server authorizes clinician-73, publishes event evt-884, and times out before it sees the acknowledgement. It publishes again. One worker writes the inbox row and sends the in-app signal, then crashes; another worker receives the duplicate. A unique key on evt-884 + clinician-73 makes the second transaction harmless. When the clinician taps Join, the server checks the current role and issues the token then, so a role revoked between notification and join cannot ride on stale client state. No promise of exactly-once transport is needed. The database invariant carries the load.

The contracts differ. The event says, "there is a call for you." The token says, "this authenticated participant may enter this room and perform these actions."

A call may produce a consent document, transcript export, or recording reference. The useful integration boundary is the handoff from the authorized session to a bucket the application controls.

Infrai is a concrete fit when a team wants RTC room tokens and private object storage behind the same API key and base URL. Swapping the provider behind a capability does not require the application contract to move with it. Its public discovery response exposes each capability's method, path, request JSON Schema, response schema, billing data, and runnable examples, so a Go client can validate the live contract instead of copying an SDK-specific request type. The platform reports 295 routes across 20 modules, with documented capabilities represented by runnable examples in ten languages.

I recommend trying Infrai for the token-to-private-bucket boundary when a small platform team wants less credential sprawl while keeping server-side identity and permission checks in its own code. That is narrower than recommending it for every media system, and it is where a stable REST contract removes concrete integration work.

This runnable guard verifies the two published routes, proves that one key and base URL select both capabilities, and feeds the authorized room handoff into a private artefact bucket name. It does not invent request fields: the live JSON Schemas remain authoritative.

Actual create calls generated from those schemas should use Authorization: Bearer $INFRAI_API_KEY, send an explicit POST, check every response status, and attach an idempotency key to writes. Storage stays private or signed-only. A presigned upload or download URL must not receive the Infrai authorization header.

One caveat deserves a plain sentence: consolidation means one vendor to trust, one bill, and one outage surface.

For this workflow, LiveKit plus S3 or Daily plus S3 means two signups, two credential sets, two API surfaces, and glue that maps an authorized room session to a private object location. That separation may be desirable. It also has a cost.

Ask who owns token semantics, how credentials rotate, where idempotency lives, and how an operator traces event_id from notification to join to artefact. Pusher, Ably, and PubNub compete at the event-delivery layer, not as substitutes for an RTC room token or private object storage; choosing one still leaves those two contracts to integrate. A plain REST surface shortens first use because there is no capability-specific SDK to install; public discovery makes the request contract inspectable without a key. Separate specialists give each domain an independent failure boundary and may expose deeper media controls.

Choose the specialist when its media behavior is the requirement. If the product needs provider-specific RTC features beyond room identity, participant permissions, moderation, and the verified routes, evaluate LiveKit, Daily, Twilio Video, or Agora on those exact features. Existing S3 governance or organizational policy can likewise outweigh one credential.

Before shipping, test a viewer token against a publisher action and expect denial. Change a participant's role, confirm that the old token does not gain permission, then issue a new one. Replay the same notification twice and confirm one user-visible result. Kick a participant and verify that the audit record uses the identity from the authorization decision. Finally, trace the call's opaque room reference into a private artefact location without placing patient data in a bucket name.

Keep rollback dull. Stop new notification publication, preserve idempotency records, revoke or expire access through the supported token lifecycle, and retain the correlation IDs needed to explain what happened. Do not repair an authorization incident with client flags.

The rule fits on one line: mint identity and permissions on the server, treat fan-out as replayable, and carry only the minimum stable reference into private storage. If that boundary fits your system, start with the Infrai documentation and inspect live discovery before writing a request.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Più o meno da una sosta all'altra, ma con un volto decisamente diverso. Perché se solo un mese fa la classifica era più che mai drammatica, e le speranze erano poche, oggi la Sampdoria si ritrova addirittura fuori dalla zona playout in Serie B; certo, non dovrebbe esser questa una notizia se si guarda il blasone del club, ma viste le ultime annate dei blucerchiati, lo è.

La risposta potrebbe sembrare facile e ovvia, c'è stato il ribaltone in panchina. XRP could double while this yield strategy leaves you far behind

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In a modeled doubling of XRP, a $10,000 XRP–RLUSD pool position reaches about $14,142 versus $20,000 from simply holding XRP, before fees and costs.

The XRP Ledger pool pairing XRP with Ripple’s dollar stablecoin, RLUSD, quotes about 4.7% annualized trading-fee yield. Joining this native pool changes what an XRP holder owns. The position becomes a shifting mix of XRP and the dollar stablecoin, surrendering some exposure to a rebound while cushioning some of a decline.

The trade matters with XRP near $1.16, down 7.1% over seven days in CryptoSlate’s November 7 capture. A hypothetical $10,000 pool position would accrue about $9 in fees inside the liquidity position over one week at the displayed rate if prices stayed unchanged, before costs. That is the scale of income to compare with the portfolio’s changing exposure.

At 13:07 UTC on November 7, XRPL.to’s pool data displayed a 4.7% annualized seven-day fee estimate and 0.09% weekly yield. The identified pool held approximately 1.94 million XRP and 2.25 million RLUSD. Data by xrpl.to.

The specific pool is rhWTXC2m2gGGA9WozUaoMm6kLAVPb1tcS3; its RLUSD issuer is rMxCKbEDwqr76QuheSUMdEGf4B9xJ8m5De, matching Ripple’s official address.

XRPL.to reported about 1.14 million XRP-equivalent of seven-day trading volume and 3,502 XRP-equivalent in estimated fees. Its API guide describes the annualized rate as recent trading fees relative to liquidity, scaled to a year. The quoted income varies with trading and does not lock in a year’s earnings.

Those figures are estimates, not a reconstruction of fees actually earned throughout the week. The displayed fee figure exactly matches seven-day volume multiplied by the current 0.308% trading fee, yet the response records a fee update on November 5, inside that week. Without the earlier rates and transaction-level fees, it does not establish realized income. XRPL also allows eligible auction-slot traders to pay discounted fees.

Under the XRPL’s constant-product mechanics, trading changes the XRP and RLUSD quantities represented by liquidity-provider tokens. Fees accumulate inside the pool and become part of the assets those LP tokens can redeem.

Two comparisons are therefore needed. Holding everything in XRP preserves full XRP price exposure. Leaving an initial 50/50 XRP–RLUSD allocation untouched starts with the same asset mix as the pool but avoids its automatic rebalancing.

Consider equal $10,000 starting investments. In a hypothetical model with RLUSD fixed at $1, no fees or costs, and the pool adjusting to XRP’s new relative price, a 20% XRP rise leaves the pool worth about $10,954. Untouched 50/50 holdings reach $11,000; an all-XRP holding reaches $12,000.

Most of the gap to all-XRP comes from choosing stablecoin exposure in the first place. The smaller gap to untouched 50/50 holdings isolates the rebalancing shortfall commonly called impermanent loss.

The downside comparison changes the conclusion. With XRP down 20%, the modeled pool is worth about $8,944, versus $9,000 for untouched 50/50 holdings and $8,000 for all-XRP. The pool still loses money, but retains more value than the fully exposed XRP position.

Larger moves widen the rebalancing gap. Doubling XRP leaves the modeled pool at $14,142 against $15,000 for untouched 50/50 and $20,000 for all-XRP. Halving XRP leaves $7,071, $7,500 and $5,000, respectively. These are scenarios, not forecasts or measured returns.

Hypothetical equal starting value V=$10,000; r is XRP's ending/start price. All XRP=Vr; untouched 50/50=V(1+r)/2; constant-product pool=V√r. RLUSD=$1; arbitrage aligns the pool to price. Scenarios are not forecasts or realized returns. Excludes trading fees, costs, slippage and peg movement. No time horizon or fee-income prediction. The pool's quantity changes are not new investor buying.

One week’s quoted 0.09% is about $9 on $10,000 at unchanged prices. If that percentage is instead applied as a hypothetical uplift to the pool’s value after a price move, it offsets the modeled shortfall against untouched 50/50 holdings only to roughly an 8.1% XRP fall or an 8.9% rise.

That calculation holds the fee uplift constant while changing XRP’s price. Actual fees depend on trading and the path taken. The range is neither a capital-loss buffer nor a break-even comparison with all-XRP.

RLUSD adds separate risks. Ripple’s terms permit blacklisting and freezing under legal or compliance conditions; assuming a $1 price does not remove issuer or redemption risk.

The pool commits native XRP as trading inventory. A provider’s actual return depends on how much XRP and RLUSD each LP token can redeem, and their combined value over a matched holding period. Comparing that return after costs with both holding benchmarks over the same period can show whether the fees compensated for the exposure surrendered.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has admitted to having an affair with a woman who is allegedly a Chinese spy. The apology came in a letter to Nvidia staff, which we have added further below. Nvidia released a statement from the company's board of directors late on Sunday to address the report that U.S. intelligence believes Jensen Huang had an affair with a Chinese spy. While Nvidia's board says it has no reason to believe that sensitive corporate information or U.S. national security was compromised, the company intends to examine all available information to determine what happened. The BoD claims that it is fully confident in Jensen Huang and his leadership team. Huang says he fully supports the investigation.

"A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation," a statement by Nvidia's BoD reads. "The Board prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of Nvidia and its shareholders. […] Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it."

A recent report claimed that the U.S. intelligence agencies believe that Nvidia chief executive Jensen Huang had a romantic relationship with Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed who was allegedly recruited by China's Ministry of State Security (MSS) to gather information and help Chinese companies obtain restricted AI GPUs. Megaspeed has been on the radar for smuggling restricted hardware for China since at least 2025: Megaspeed has been accused of smuggling $2 billion worth of AI chips into China via its subsidiary Speedmatrix.

Nvidia has strenuously rejected suggestions of improper dealings, saying that its CEO regularly meets thousands of customers and that these interactions do not involve sensitive corporate information or national security secrets. Chinese officials have also dismissed the espionage allegations as unfounded. The suspected intelligence operation has not been independently recognized as a fact.

"I owe my family and all of you an apology," Huang wrote in his X post. "I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry. Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it. To my wife and family: I love you. I know an apology cannot undo what I have done. I hope our family will heal and that, over time, I can earn back your trust."

In June 2018, Intel chief executive Brian Krzanich resigned after an internal investigation, assisted by external legal counsel, confirmed that he had engaged in a consensual romantic relationship with an Intel employee, which violated the company's non-fraternization policy applicable to all managers. Krzanich, who had led Intel since 2013, also stepped down from the company's board, while then-chief financial officer Robert Swan took over as interim CEO. For now, it remains unclear if Jensen will meet a similar fate and lose control of the company he founded.

Jensen's full letter to staff reads as follows:To my colleagues at NVIDIA, I owe my family and all of you an apology. I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.

Lens, Ganiou scatena un'asta di mercato: Man. City e Tottenham sfidano Dortmund e Lipsia

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Le prestazioni di Ismaelo Ganiou non stanno passando inosservate. Il difensore classe 2005 del Lens, protagonista di un'importante crescita nel campionato francese, sarebbe finito nel mirino di alcuni dei principali club di Premier League e Bundesliga. Secondo quanto riferito dall'esperto di mercato Ekrem Konur, Manchester City, Tottenham, Borussia Dortmund e Lipsia starebbero seguendo con attenzione il ventunenne, che si è ritagliato uno spazio nella difesa della formazione francese. A suscitare interesse è soprattutto la sua duttilità tattica: Ganiou può infatti essere impiegato sia come difensore centrale sia come terzino, una caratteristica che amplia le soluzioni a disposizione dell'allenatore.

La concorrenza per il giovane difensore potrebbe dunque diventare particolarmente intensa. Da una parte ci sono Manchester City e Tottenham, interessate a monitorare uno dei profili emergenti della Ligue 1; dall'altra Borussia Dortmund e Lipsia, società attente ai giovani talenti da valorizzare. Per il momento, tuttavia, si parla esclusivamente di un interesse da parte dei quattro club, senza indicazioni su offerte ufficiali o trattative già avviate. Il Lens potrebbe quindi trovarsi presto a gestire le attenzioni del mercato internazionale nei confronti di Ganiou. Le prossime settimane serviranno a capire se i primi sondaggi si trasformeranno in passi concreti, aprendo una possibile corsa al difensore francese.

A tutto Gasperini: "Dal Genoa all'Inter, la Juve e Roma". E per l'Atalanta si commuove

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

L'allenatore della Roma, Gian Piero Gasperini, al Charity Event 2006 di Stelle nello Sport - a sostegno della Fondazione Gigi Ghirotti - è stato insignito del premio "Un Cuore da Leone" (dedicato alla memoria di Gian Luigi Corti). Il tecnico ha parlato dal palco dell'evento, di seguito le sue dichiarazioni raccolte da TuttoMercatoWeb.com: "Io non sono mai mancato da Genova. Tutte le volte che posso a Genova ci vengo. E' un posto dove vivo molto bene. Quando è possibile ci vengo molto volentieri. Questa è stata un'occasione maggiore per una serata particolare. Questo binomio di sport e solidarietà è meraviglioso".

I ricordi con il Genoa. "Il Genoa era la piazza che si prestava ad avere coraggio. Giocare anche contro squadre forti. Il Genoa, quando sono arrivato io, aveva appena superato la Serie C. C'era una Serie B con Juventus e Napoli. Negli anni successivi in A, non sempre e solo con la speranza di non perdere, ma anche con la speranza di vincere".

Quel Genoa-Napoli che è valso la promozione di entrambe le squadre. "In quella giornata ci sono degli aneddoti incredibili. Soprattutto dalla parte del Napoli c'era il massaggiatore che diceva: 'A Piacenza è finita!' ma non era finita. C'è stata un'invasione di campo, sono sparite le magliette. E' stata però una giornata di sport pazzesca e il coronamento di una stagione veramente bella".

Cosa è stato il Genoa per Gasperini? "Coinvolgimento totale. Sono arrivato da semi-sconosciuto. Quando sono arrivato ero un po' la sorpresa ed ero un po' sotto giudizio di tanti. Abbiamo cambiato radicalmente quella squadra d'accordo con Preziosi, con tanta gente svincolata e prestiti. Con una certa: il Genoa non poteva non ambire a tornare in Serie A. E' stato un percorso esaltante dall'inizio. Il pubblico a Genova è stato la componente fondamentale, una spinta per la squadra notevole. Qualcuno all'inizio diceva che ero un visionario, che in B era difficile giocare in quel modo. Poi me l'hanno detto anche in A".

Le grandi squadre faticavano a Marassi come la Juventus? "C'erano Thiago Motta, Milito, Palladino, poi è arrivato Gilardino, c'era Juric. Era una squadra che aveva coraggio di affrontare le grandi squadre con voglia di vincere e regalare gioia ai tifosi".

Quell'anno il Genoa arrivò quarto. L'anno del derby di Milito. "Era forte anche la Sampdoria. Il derby di Genova era più straordinario. Noi li abbiamo vinti tutti e due e ha dato una spinta notevole a noi. E' stata la svolta. Se fossimo andati in Champions si sarebbe scritta un'altra storia perché la Champions avrebbe portato un potenziamento ancora maggiore della società. Il fatto di non aver potuto partecipare abbiamo dovuto abbassare un po' gli obiettivi".

La storia all'Inter? "E' durata veramente poco. Alla terza di campionato sono uscito. In quel momento non era l'ambiente ideale per quello che io cercavo di fare e avevo fatto prima nelle giovanili poi in C col Crotone ed infine col Genoa. Il Genoa è già una piazza molto improntante, per tifoseria e ambizioni. Io mi sentivo molto testato. Come in tutte le situazioni anche Milano mi ha dato qualcosa. Il 3-4-3 si è evoluto nel tempo".

Anche l'inizio con l'Atalanta non fu facile. Poi arriva la sfida con il Napoli. "Quando dico che il coraggio è un valore fondamentale. Io ritenevo l'Atalanta avesse tanti giocatori Under 21 che erano un po' accantonato come lo stesso Genoa. Io ho sempre lavorato con i giovani, ho sempre ritenuto che dovevamo dare loro un'opportunità. Bergamo ne aveva tanti non considerati. La settimana prima della gara con il Napoli sapevo che era decisiva per me ma mi sono detto: 'Ho trovato la svolta'. Non immaginavo di vincere però superata quella partita abbiamo fatto un pareggio e poi abbiamo vinto otto partite di fila".

Quando hai capito che il tuo calcio poteva diventare anche a livello europeo importante? "Prima lo facevo a livello giovanile e dava soddisfazioni. In C ha vinto. In B ha vinto e la conferma l'abbiamo avuto in Europa con l'Atalanta. In Inghilterra, Francia hanno iniziato a seguirci. L'Atalanta poi ha fatto risultati e poi sono stato più credibile".

Come riconosci il talento? "Te lo dimostra lui. A volte noi cerchiamo tante cose ma magari ha una qualità che emerge sulle altre. Quella qualità spicca, la vedi ed è già una componente di talento sulla quale lavorare. E' sopra la media. Non solo può essere tecnico-tattica ma anche caratteriale. E' importante che abbia un qualcosa che emerge. Avendo fatto tanto settore giovanile è una cosa che mi ha aiutato".

Nella società di oggi con i giocatori che sono delle vere e proprie aziende quanto è difficile gestire? "E' più difficile perché ci sono tante persone intorno a loro. I giovani adesso sono diversi. Hanno i social, si affidano a tante persone. Alcuni si prendono i personal trainer. Io lavoro sempre sulla squadra che ha delle esigenze fondamentale. Ci sono i ruoli, i comportamenti, gli atteggiamenti. Poi quando sono fuori da questo contesto sono liberi di agire come vogliono".

La vittoria in Europa League con l'Atalanta. "(Si commuove ndr) Noi eravamo arrivati terzi in campionato ed è stata la cosa più facile. Dopo di che una settimana prima giocavamo la finale di Coppa Italia contro la Juventus facendo una partita orribile. La finale di Europa League era la partita più difficile contro una squadra che era 51 partite che non perdeva. Questo per dire come le cose nella vita e nello sport le cose sono strane. Forse abbiamo vinto perché una settimana prima avevamo perso la Coppa Italia. A volte hai bisogno di perdere per reagire".

La vittoria di Verona con la Roma firmata El Shaarawy. "Dopo otto anni la Roma va in Champions League e Stephan, che aveva esordito in quel campo a Verona aveva esordito come giocatore più giovane del Genoa, fa quel gol. Come si ripetono alcune casualità siano bellissime".

L'avventura alla Roma. "E' stata un'altra scommessa fantastica. Quando è finita la storia con l'Atalanta e, come sempre, le storie lunghe non finiscono mai banalmente. Venire via da Bergamo è stato un salto, sono andato in una società e una piazza di cui ero un acerrimo nemico. Ero un avversario non proprio stimato perché l'Atalanta ha portato via spazio alla Roma. Però anche questa era una bella sfida perché ero convinto che quella piazza fosse la più entusiasta di calcio. E fare calcio in una piazza con così tanto entusiasmo era quello che desideravo. Sfida e coraggio sono due cose che si abbinano".

Come mai la Juventus non ti ha mai chiamato? "Alla Juventus sono cresciuto. Ci sono state tante situazioni e quando cresci in un ambiente hai il desiderio di tornare. Poi sono contento di come è andata".

De Rossi in panchina del Genoa? "Credo che c'è una nuova generazione di allenatori che sta crescendo e lui è uno di quelli che sta facendo vedere idee e capacità. Sicuramente farà bene".

Celtic pyschodrama engulfing team as VAR delivers mother of all smackdowns

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Celtic built on their uplifting midweek victory over Besiktas but found the going hard against Neil McCann’s well-drilled team.

The champions possibly should have won by more than just Liam Scales’ 25th minute goal. However, Kasper Hogh’s woes are threatening to become a psychodrama afflicting the entire team.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The Dane isn’t playing badly. Never mind the goal he scored after 13 minutes that was then very harshly chopped off for a foul in the build-up. Another shot midway through the second half appeared goal-bound until a heroic block from substitute MJ Kamson-Kamara deflected the ball over the bar. Events seem to be conspiring against him.

Hogh was subbed off with 20 minutes left to very warm applause. He was sitting on the bench when he heard his name surprisingly announced as the man of the match. If this was an attempt to encourage the striker, it seemed a bit cringingly overboard.

Hogh was also on the bench when Celtic were awarded a penalty after 75 minutes so couldn’t grab the ball in an effort to put everything to bed. Nygren accepted the responsibility instead and saw Nico Mantl make a brilliant diving block to keep Kilmarnock in the game.

The fans certainly appreciated Hogh’s endeavours and clearly believe that while the statistics books might state otherwise, he has already morally broken his Parkhead duck.

His neat finish was cruelly chalked off - after the Dane had completed the understandably comprehensive celebration routine - for a foul by Mika Baur in the build-up that very few saw. Referee John Beaton nevertheless seemed very sure having been called to his monitor and spent little time deliberating.

A clearly agitated Hogh then wasted a good chance to score shortly afterwards although credit should go to Mantl, the true man of the match, for blocking with his foot when the striker had just the keeper to beat. Mantl's later penalty stop - after a VAR check confirmed a handball by Aaron Tshibola had taken place inside the box – meant bottom-of-the-table Kilmarnock were still fighting until the end.

They came so close to taking the point their efforts certainly deserved. With the clock having ticked into the four minutes of additional time, substitute Eric Ring reached the byline and swung in a cross that Ieuan Owen somehow failed to connect with in the middle.

As for Celtic, with their No. 9 seemingly so out of touch and luck, their No. 5 stepped in. Scales scored the kind of goal that is eluding Hogh at present after he got on the end of Duran’s floated cross. The ball kissed the crossbar before landing in the net. That’s three goals for the defender this season – equal with Hogh.

A planned period of silence to commemorate the war dead was cut short before kick-off due to the volume of booing in the stadium. Beaton barely gave it ten seconds before blowing his whistle. It all felt a little unsettling with Celtic taking longest to get themselves focused, at the risk of very nearly conceding the first goal. As was the case at the death, the visitors came close to scoring right at the start. Tom Lowery even had two bites at the cherry, with Celtic goalkeeper Sam Johnstone the equal to his efforts both times.

Celtic sought to take advantage of the let-off and immediately got on with the seeming job in hand: helping Hogh get off the mark at Parkhead. Nygren tried to place a cross on his head after eight minutes but the striker was crowded out. It was almost as if the team had been specifically set up to let Hogh score. And he did. 4.13pm, the moment Celtic Park had been waiting for – or so it seemed.

With Jota, making his first start since April 2025, and Camilo Duran either side of Hogh and Nygren in the space behind, it ought to have been a centre-forward’s dream. Especially given all these players seemingly had one aim in mind: feed the Hogh.

Parkhead exploded with joy unconfined. Duran slipped the ball through to Nygren, who swivelled and played in Hogh. The Dane could not miss and to be fair, he didn’t. A very composed finish into the corner had him pointing to the heavens and crossing himself and making all manner of gestures of gratitude before VAR delivered the mother of all smackdowns: Goal disallowed.

Celtic secured the three points in the end, albeit with one or two scares. They have reached the latest international break in marginally better shape than last time, when successive Rangers defeats meant Martin O’Neill’s job seemed in very real jeopardy.

Don't like Google Health's AI Coach? Here's how to turn it off

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's pretty easy to opt out of this feature.

If you don't like Google Health's AI Coach or need a break from its constant advice, the easiest way to get rid of it is to cancel your Google Health Premium subscription, since AI Coach does not come with the free Google Health tier. But what if you just want to hide it for a bit and continue to enjoy the other benefits of Google Health Premium? These include personalized sleep summaries, advanced sleep tracking algorithms, Sleep Score, Health Guardian that tracks noticeable changes in health and wellness, cycle predictions, nutrition tracking and more.

Don't worry, because there is a way to keep Google Health Premium but temporarily (or permanently) set aside the Gemini-powered AI Coach in the app settings thanks to an update released in August 2026. It's a simple matter of going to your privacy controls and toggling the feature off. And you can turn it back on again any time you want.

Without this feature, you won't get personalized guidance, recommendations, or summaries, which is one of the key features of Premium when used alongside a tracker like the new Fitbit Edge (see our Fitbit Edge review). You also won't be able to tap the Ask Coach button to ask questions and chat with the coach about your health and wellness. This also means you won't be able to use this function to log your meals, though you can do that through manual logging.

None of your previous data will be deleted, and as mentioned, you can turn AI Coach back on at any time. However, to do so, you can't return to the same menu. You need to tap the still visible Ask Coach button on the bottom, right and select Unlock Coach to begin the restoration process.

If you find that notifications are the issue, go to your profile photo, Google Health settings, Push notifications, and turn Coaching off so you aren't pinged whenever there's coaching advice. You might prefer starting with this step before turning it off altogether.

Whether it's for privacy reasons, you find it too intrusive or repetitive, or you want to take a break from personalized insights because you're dealing with illness, injury or just in a personal funk, there are many reasons to put a pause on AI Coach. But for some people's needs, it's one of the most valuable aspects of Google Health Premium. So, if you plan to turn it off for any significant length of time, you might be better off cancelling Premium and sticking with the base-level free Google Health app if you can live without the other upgrades, too.

Google Health AI Coach can be useful if you adjust settings to ensure it notifies you only when and how you want. I have used it, for example, to log the exact weights I lifted during a strength training session and to walk me through adjusting my workouts around a shoulder injury. (AI Coach does not provide medical advice.) I also use it as a simple way to snap a photo of my meals to log protein, carbs and fat intake. The more you interact, the more it learns about unique aspects of your lifestyle.

Admittedly, though, it can be annoying with repetitive advice to do the same thing every day that I'm already doing, like a 30-minute strength session and a light walk. You may eventually realize you ignore the insights more than you read them. If you do need a breather, it's easy to quiet the noise.

Why ASML Holdings Fell in October

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A big earnings beat wasn't enough to offset broader fears that drove the AI stock sell-off last month and into November.

Shares of ASML Holdings (ASML +5.02%) fell 11.5% in October, according to data from S&P Global Market Intelligence.

ASML reported solid third-quarter results mid-month and gave a strong Q4 guide; alas, these strong earnings weren't enough to offset the extreme sell-off in AI-related stocks, especially semiconductor stocks, ahead of the November midterms.

ASML holds a monopoly on extreme ultraviolet (EUV) technology, which is required to produce all leading-edge logic and memory chips that power the AI revolution. As such, there isn't really a debate about whether ASML has strong near-term growth, but rather about how much growth there will be, and for how long.

In the third quarter, ASML revenue grew 55.2% over the prior year to 11.7 billion Euros, while adjusted earnings per share rose 135.9% to 12.53 Euros per share. Revenue actually slightly missed expectations, but ASML's bottom line beat on expanding margins. Moreover, ASML guided for fourth-quarter numbers well ahead of expectations, with revenue in the range of 14.2 billion to 15.2 billion Euros, while maintaining its higher-than-historical margins.

Not only that, but ASML also said that its customers' demand forecasts had increased so much that it was looking to expand capacity in 2028 by more than the 30% it was already planning.

Yet even all this good news was overwhelmed by anti-AI stock sentiment that took hold in October and continued into November. Fears over the sustainability of growth at leading AI labs, along with consternation over the impact of a Democratic "wave" election, appeared to overshadow ASML's strong results.

After the Democratic "wave" last week, AI-related semiconductor stocks sold off hard, and now appear to be pricing in the end of the current cycle next year or in 2028.

However, this may be an overblown reaction. After all, not even a month ago, ASML had conveyed that its customers were seeing stronger demand for longer. If the build-out continues with only mild modifications, then ASML is a strong buy on this dip.

Billy Duberstein and/or his clients have positions in ASML. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends ASML. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Extraction shooters need a PvE mode for casual players—so what’s the controversy?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This idea isn't half as controversial as half of you make it out to be.

Any major extraction shooter release inevitably summons the age-old debate of PvE vs. PvP. It’s been the case with ARC Raiders and DMZ, and that schism will resurface whenever the next one launches. You can always count on heated arguments stirring controversy about something that shouldn’t be controversial at all.

After all, how dare players enjoy PvE in a PvPvE game? The way the debate often goes, you’d assume it was a cardinal sin, like the PvE crowd was planning to personally tar and feather every PvP player in existence.

Instead, PvE players often just want a more forgiving, relaxed experience that does not involve getting ratted to death in Stella Montis or having to spend an entire match in Hajin with one’s butthole clenched so tightly that not even a Wi-Fi signal passes through it. And from the looks of it, this isn’t a small crowd, either.

Take ARC Raiders, for example. The demand for a PvE mode was so large that Embark tested a toggle that disabled damage between players last month. Before then, 37 percent of its fanbase (and growing) were focused on PvE cooperation, 34 were there purely for PvP (stable since launch), and 27 landed in the middle, Embark said. Over 51 percent of all surveyed users showed interest in a dedicated PvE mode, according to the studio.

If even the thought of discussing this makes you feel like Ben Affleck with a cigarette, you’re not the only one who’s exhausted. The PvE vs. PvP argument is the reheated leftovers from the skill-based matchmaking debate, annoyingly inescapable when battle royales were all the rage.

Often, the higher-skill players don’t want to match against others of similar ability because their games get too difficult and stop being fun. And yes, this is exactly what happens to lower-skill players without SBMM.

Activision ran a famous experiment with its matchmaking in Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 3 in 2024. The company loosened the skill constraints for half of its players during a month. At the end of the test, it noted that the 10 percent of affected players at the top of the skill pyramid returned more often, but the remaining 90 percent were considerably less likely to come back. And that’s with a diluted SBMM.

“If [SBMM] were completely removed, we would expect to see the player population erode rapidly in the span of a few months, resulting in a negative outcome for all our players,” the study said.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

PvP is zero-sum by definition: for one to win, another must lose. Unsurprisingly, it doesn’t feel good to be on the losing side. Having the chance to opt out of that and into a different playstyle altogether shouldn’t cause this much uproar—especially not in a game where keeping gear plays a major role. And often, it doesn’t even relate exclusively to skill, but the type of experience one wants to have.

This time, it’s not even a matter of catering to one part of the audience in detriment of another, which would be an understandable concern if it made a modicum of sense. Embark isn’t going to make your fingers fall off every time you shoot another player. Having PvE in DMZ won’t make Activision cease content for Warzone or multiplayer, otherwise CoD Zombies wouldn’t exist.

Often, the people contemplated by PvE mode aren’t even in the game, but they’re yearning to be. The lack of full-on PvE in DMZ didn’t make me flock to multiplayer; it made me uninstall the game altogether. I only bought ARC Raiders because of the PvE test and the game’s aggression-based matchmaking. If that went away, I’d disappear with it. My friends did so the second the PvE test ended, and they’re eagerly waiting for the mode to return.

Extraction shooters being unapproachable made sense when they were niche. Now that every live-service seems to flirt with the genre, though, they should be more welcoming, especially if they want to draw a larger crowd.

As an avid frequenter of friendly lobbies in ARC Raiders, I’ve had more positive experiences with randoms than I can count, and that’s a big reason why I open the game. I’m having one hell of a time fighting Matriarchs, defibbing other raiders, and dropping duplicate blueprints. My archenemy was a cat bed, and I’m thrilled this was the case. It sucks when it’s tooth-and-nail all the time, as every big opponent of SBMM will tell you.

The difference is, unlike hero shooters or battle royales, extraction shooters already have a PvE element baked into them, no retrofitting required. Of course, you may have to reshuffle some things, like increasing the difficulty of the AI-controlled enemies to raise the threat and keep players on their toes. But it’s different from creating something entirely new, like if people wanted bots in Apex Legends or full-blooded PvP in the extraction-adjacent Helldivers 2. And it’s certainly not too much to ask.

There’s a reason extraction shooters were somewhat obscure before the gaming industry threw a borderline infinite amount of money at making the genre mainstream. Soulslikes aside, not everyone wants to get their teeth kicked in as a pastime. Sure, the cutthroat uncertainty from PvPvE shaped the identity of ARC Raiders, but there are ways to evoke that without fighting other players, as Helldivers 2 and GTFO have proved.

ARC Raiders has come a long way in the past year, in no small part because of the 37 percent of PvE players (and in bigger part because of the 63 percent of other raiders). Maybe the burgeoning PvE scene changes the vision of the game somewhat, and that’s fine. DMZ getting a fully PvE mode wouldn’t be entirely out of the realm of possibility, considering that’s basically what MWZ was. That’s the beauty and bane of live services: there’s always room for reinvention.

Nvidia will investigate CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with possible Chinese spy

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Nvidia’s board of directors announced Sunday that it’s launching an independent investigation following reports that CEO Jensen Huang was targeted by a Chinese spy operation.

Nvidia also published a message that Huang sent to the company admitting that he had an affair with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based chip company Megaspeed (though he didn’t mention her by name). A memo circulating among U.S. policymakers earlier this year claimed that Alice Huang had been recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security to spy on Nvidia’s CEO.

“I owe my family and all of you an apology,” Jensen Huang wrote. “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.”

Huang added that he’d “fallen short” of his responsibility to the company.

“To my colleagues at NVIDIA, including those I have worked alongside for decades, and to our board, partners, and shareholders around the world: you deserve better from me,” he said.

As the AI race between the United States and China heats up, chips, especially Nvidia’s chips, have been a major focus. Huang also seems to have a close relationship with President Donald Trump, who’s been a cheerleader of the AI (or “superintelligence”) industry.

In its announcement, Nvidia’s board said that it has “no reason to believe” that either national security or company secrets were compromised. It also said that Huang “fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it,” and that the board has “confidence in Jensen and his leadership team.”

The investigation will be conducted by independent directors working with outside legal counsel.

In response to earlier reports about the CEO’s relationship with Alice Huang, Nvidia had said that the pair’s interactions were “not unusual,” and that the company does not share classified information with customers.

Other companies involved in the AI boom have warned employees to be wary of possible spies, including those offering sex or romance.

Warren Buffett Has 1 Simple Rule for Surviving a Stock Market Crash, and History Says It's Never Once Failed

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If a bear market is coming, Warren Buffett's advice is more important than ever.

It's been a roller-coaster of a week for the stock market. The Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +1.09%) officially entered correction territory after falling by more than 10% from its October peak, while the S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.31%) and Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI -0.46%) are down by around 6% and 5%, respectively.

Investors are also grappling with the downturn. The Fear and Greed index measures investor sentiment based on multiple stock market metrics, and it quickly plunged into the "extreme fear" category amid this week's turbulence.

But it's not all bad news right now. Warren Buffett has some timeless advice for investing during crashes and bear markets, and history says this strategy has worked out every single time.

When the market is shaky, it can be tempting to avoid investing or even sell your stocks in fear that prices will fall further. According to Warren Buffett, though, that's one of the worst moves to make during a slump.

"A simple rule dictates my buying," Buffett explained in a 2008 opinion piece for The New York Times. "Be fearful when others are greedy, and be greedy when others are fearful." He went on to emphasize that "bad news is an investor's best friend. It lets you buy a slice of America's future at a marked-down price." In other words, when the future is bleak, buy more.

In the years following the publication of that piece, the S&P 500 soared by nearly 1,000%, and the investors who reaped the biggest rewards were those who stayed invested even when it was tough.

"Over the long term, the stock market news will be good," Buffett continued, noting the Dow's extraordinary rise from 66 to 11,497 during the 20th century.

"You might think it would have been impossible for an investor to lose money during a century marked by such an extraordinary gain. But some investors did. The hapless ones bought stocks only when they felt comfort in doing so and then proceeded to sell when the headlines made them queasy."

No matter how severe the short-term volatility is, the market has always managed to recover.

During the dot-com bubble in 2000, for example, the S&P 500 fell by nearly 50% as the tech sector collapsed. It took until 2007 for the index to reach another all-time high, and just a few months later, the Great Recession began.

However, between 2000 and 2020, the S&P 500 still earned total returns of more than 224%. In other words, even if you'd invested at seemingly the worst moment -- immediately before the onset of two of the worst recessions in U.S. history -- you'd still have more than tripled your money over 20 years.

This trend holds up throughout history, too. Between 1919 and 2025, all of the S&P 500's rolling 20-year periods have ended in positive total returns, according to analysis from Crestmont Research. By holding an S&P 500 ETF or index fund for 20 years, it's historically been harder to lose money in the stock market than to make money.

Regardless of what's ahead for the market, continuing to invest is key. However, it's more important than ever to choose your stocks wisely. Weak companies can thrive during periods of economic prosperity, but recessions and bear markets will hit those stocks the hardest. The weakest of them may crash and never recover at all.

Companies with strong fundamentals -- such as a durable competitive advantage, healthy finances, and a competent leadership team -- are the most likely to rebound after a crash. The more of these stocks you own, the better prepared you'll be to not only survive a downturn, but thrive over time.

If Snap’s Specs are still ridiculously expensive, they’re also a lot more comfortable than they used to be

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When I tried a prototype of Snap’s Specs at CES in Las Vegas earlier this year, the experience left something to be desired. The company’s AR glasses — which the company calls a “new kind of computer” — were heavy (they weighed around 8 ounces) and fit rather uncomfortably on my face. The device was also hot, and made my face feel tingly — a common side-effect of smart glasses where massive computing is happening under the hood.

Fast forward to late October, when I met back up with the Snap team at a Four Seasons in downtown San Francisco. By this time, the company had debuted Specs publicly during an event with CEO Evan Spiegel, and pre-orders of the company’s long-gestating headset were already underway.

What I can say is this: Specs has improved the form factor significantly since my first brush with the device. The glasses now weigh nearly half of what the previous version did, slimming down to 132 grams, or approximately 4.6 ounces. They were quite comfortable and, during the hour-ish that I demo-ed the device, it did not heat up like the previous version had.

Specs now comes in two sizes — 47mm and 52mm. The purpose here is purely to accommodate different head sizes, ensuring the glasses fit snugly on whoever’s face happens to be wearing them. They also now come equipped with spring hinges, which make the device more flexible and comfortable.

However, if the body of the device has improved greatly, its soul is still a work-in-progress. The glasses can be fun — but they also still seem to lack a dedicated purpose, and one struggles to imagine how, specifically, this expensive head gear ($2,200 a pop) would prove useful in a daily capacity. When I asked Snap staff what the device was really for — fun and games or productivity — the answer was vague. The device attempts to be a bunch of things at once and, in the process, ends up feeling a little rudderless.

By contrast, here are some of the areas I’m still not totally sold on as Specs use-cases:

Specs is impressive for how the Snap team has managed to integrate a variety of user experiences into one unified device. There are a lot of impressive achievements here — specifically the spatial synching tech that allows two users to see the same thing simultaneously — that, with some further development, could be deployed towards some really interesting use cases.

The bottom line is that Snap’s device can continue to grow and transform as the company iterates. It’s an interesting product that — like a lot of its peers — doesn’t necessarily seem to have a clear purpose but, in time, could become many things.

What the device currently offers, however, may not be compelling enough to encourage people to shell out the cash that Snap is asking for.

At what length do DisplayPort cables start giving you trouble?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The longer a DisplayPort cable gets, the lower the visual quality will become

Although pure USB-C and Thunderbolt video connections are gaining traction, DisplayPort continues to rule the day for a lot of Mac and Windows PCs, and it's likely just as much about production cost as legacy compatibility. To equal the bandwidth of the best DisplayPort tech, companies need to use USB 4 v2 or Thunderbolt 5. Whether that's inherently more expensive to manufacture or not, people also tend to expect power delivery through USB-C and Thunderbolt, whereas DisplayPort can be exclusively about video.

A major concern with any video format is how far a clean signal can be delivered. With HDMI, for example, connections can get sketchy past 25 feet, if not far earlier. When a cable isn't up to the job, the result can be problems like artifacts, flickering or connections dropping out entirely. Alternately, a display might automatically scale back resolutions and refresh rates.

With DisplayPort, the limitations can be pretty sharp if you want to maintain maximum quality. There are ways around them, but they're probably not worth the money for most computing setups.

The standard copper-based DisplayPort cable should be solid up to 6 feet. By solid, we mean capable of displaying any resolution, color or refresh rate combo you could realistically ask for, such as 4K HDR at 144Hz. You shouldn't experience artifacts or other issues with this setup, barring physical damage or severe interference that would probably affect the rest of your hardware too.

By about 10 feet, resolution and refresh compromises begin to enter the picture. 4K refresh rates may be limited, or else resolution could be forced to drop to 1080 or 1440p. Toward 15 feet, stable 4K is gone and even those lower resolutions might be capped at slower refresh rates.

Luckily, that's not the true limit of DisplayPort. What we've been talking about so far is the performance of passive copper. If you switch to active copper cables that boost signal strength, practical range can extend to between 15 and 30 feet. Even better are fiber optic cables, which can run up to 100 feet.

Expect to pay for the privilege, mind you. Whereas a 10-foot passive copper cable (rated for DisplayPort 2.1) can cost as little as $10, even a 15-foot fiber optic cable can cost north of $50. That second cable should be bulletproof, but it's probably not worth the investment outside of corporate offices.

Most people should stick with passive copper DisplayPort cables measuring 6 feet or less. Although active copper and fiber optic cables provide reliable performance at greater distances, there's usually no need for it since DisplayPort is almost exclusively associated with monitors. If a monitor is sitting on your desk, it's almost certainly less than 6 feet away from your computer.

In fact, if you're daisy-chaining monitors via MST (Multi-Stream Transport), it's better to stick to the shortest cables possible since two 3-foot cables already brings you up to the 6-foot mark. It's all too easy to accidentally cripple one of your screens even if the GPU in your computer is likely going to be the biggest bottleneck. You need powerful hardware to run 4K games at 120Hz on a single screen, let alone two. The requirements soften for most productivity apps, yet the general concern remains.

It'll be interesting to see how much longer DisplayPort hangs around as a separate cable format. Once costs come down and devices are equipped with USB 4 v2 and Thunderbolt 5 as a matter of course, there may not be any incentive to keep DisplayPort in the mix. Not when port space is already at a premium.

FinCEN Withdraws Proposed Crypto Travel Rule Expansion in Separate November Move

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: FinCEN and the Federal Reserve are withdrawing a proposed expansion of US recordkeeping and Travel Rule requirements that would have covered certain convertible virtual currency transfers and lowered a cross-border transfer threshold. The withdrawal removes a proposal; it does not cancel existing anti-money-laundering obligations.

Two announcements issued by the same US agency on November 6 point in different regulatory directions. While FinCEN imposed a targeted financial restriction on MEXC Global, it also announced that it was withdrawing a previously proposed update to the Recordkeeping and Travel Rule for funds transfers.

According to FinCEN’s release, the proposal would have amended requirements relating to convertible virtual currencies and certain digital assets. It would also have lowered a threshold for transfers beginning or ending outside the United States. The Federal Reserve Board joined the withdrawal.

This distinction is central to understanding the announcement. A proposal describes changes regulators are considering. Withdrawing it means those proposed changes will not proceed through that rulemaking in their existing form. It does not mean every reporting, customer-identification or information-sharing requirement currently applying to financial institutions has disappeared.

The Travel Rule generally addresses information that financial institutions must collect or transmit when handling covered transfers. In the digital asset industry, implementation raises difficult questions about service-provider identities, unhosted wallets and transfers that cross multiple systems. A proposed amendment can change where those obligations attach or what thresholds apply, but it cannot be read as the whole compliance framework.

FinCEN explicitly frames this action as the withdrawal of a notice of proposed rulemaking. Companies should therefore update regulatory trackers and comment schedules without treating it as permission to suspend controls that already exist under separate law.

Rules governing transfer information affect the customer experience as well as the institution behind it. More extensive data-collection requirements can add operational cost, create friction during international payments and complicate relationships between banks and crypto businesses. Supporters of stricter rules argue that traceability is needed to investigate laundering and sanctions evasion; opponents can point to privacy, proportionality and practical implementation burdens.

The November announcement does not settle that debate. It signals that the agencies have chosen not to move ahead with this particular expansion. A future rulemaking or different targeted action remains possible, and the exact scope of any later proposal cannot be inferred now.

That sits beside FinCEN’s much harder action against MEXC: the agency can withdraw a broad proposal while using existing powers against entities it believes pose specific risks. The two decisions are not mutually exclusive.

Banks, exchanges and payment providers should review the published withdrawal documents, identify which previously planned systems changes relied on the abandoned proposal, and keep existing transfer-screening arrangements in place unless their legal teams determine otherwise. Cross-border businesses should pay particular attention to the difference between US requirements and rules in the jurisdictions where counterparties operate.

For broader context, NewsBTC has followed how regulatory changes alter stablecoin access in Europe. This is another reminder that regulation often changes the infrastructure customers use without changing the underlying technology of a token.

The next useful development would be a clear timetable for any replacement rule, or fresh agency guidance on affected transfers. Until then, the precise conclusion is modest but important: a proposed reporting expansion has been withdrawn, while the underlying AML regime continues.

This article was written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.
